==============================================================================
APPENDIX (IDF objects): information in the High-rise Apartment IDF that is NOT in the input documents
==============================================================================
Target file: cases/doe_high_rise_apartment/traceability/full/reference_full.idf (DOE/PNNL prototype "High-rise Apartment", ASHRAE 90.1-2004, climate zone 5A, Buffalo NY, EnergyPlus 23.1 syntax).
Documents: data/DOE/inputs/PNNL_Prototype_Scorecards.xlsx (called "xlsx") and data/DOE/inputs/U.S. Department of Energy Commercial Reference Building Models of.pdf (called "PDF").
Generated by: a script of the benchmark authors (not in this repository) with the configuration of the building (run it again after any change of the IDF).
Companion file: Appendix_en.md (words and tables). Use it together with this file. Appendix_IDF_full.txt is this file plus the objects that were moved to Appendix_en.md.

1. PURPOSE
----------
The documents describe the building: areas, multipliers, occupancy, lighting and plug densities, infiltration, outdoor air, hourly schedule values, the HVAC system TYPE, window U and SHGC, and the wall, roof and slab TYPE. They do not contain the geometry, the layer data of the constructions, the HVAC equipment, the curves, the controls, the simulation settings or the output requests.
If you do not have the original prototype IDF file, the items below that are not in the documents are the assumptions to use to rebuild the IDF.
This file holds the IDF text that cannot be written in words: the geometry (zones, surfaces, openings), the node connections and all wiring objects, the HVAC objects with their node names, the schedules of the xlsx with their day-type structure, the loads and the output requests. The object classes whose values are given completely in Appendix_en.md are not repeated; a comment line "! [moved to Appendix_en.md section x: class]" marks the place of each removed run of objects, in the order of the IDF. To build the IDF, write those objects from the tables of Appendix_en.md at the marked place (the object syntax is that of EnergyPlus 23.1; the field order is that of the Input Output Reference).

2. HOW TO BUILD THE IDF
----------------------
How to build the IDF from the documents and this file:
a. Take the body (everything after the line "BEGIN IDF BODY") as the IDF text.
b. Every marker @DOC[x]@ stands for a value that comes from the documents. The comment on the same line (after "Source:") names the xlsx cell or row, or the PDF table. Read the value there, convert it to SI (section 3) and write it in place of the marker. The number x inside the brackets is the value the finished IDF has; use it to check your reading and your conversion. The value itself must come from the document. Some markers are computed from a document value, as the comment says: the number of the people of a zone is not used (the xlsx gives the floor area per person, raw_data M); the outdoor air per floor area comes from the sheet "Zone and HVAC System Summary" (not from raw_data P, which lists 0 where the outdoor air is set at the air loop).
c. Names are never markers. Object, zone and schedule names are written exactly as in the IDF (the zone "G SW Apartment" is "G SW APARTMENT" in the xlsx; the IDF spelling is the one every other object refers to).
d. Comments (text after "!" or "!-") are provenance only; EnergyPlus ignores them. Most comments of the original IDF were removed: the citation header, section headings, commented-out objects, and the "Default:" and "Kept:" notes. The notes that give a document location ("Source: xlsx ..." or "Source: NREL PDF ...") remain.
d2. A field that is empty in the body takes the EnergyPlus 23.1 default. In the original IDF such a field held a value equal to that default, so the model does not change. Fields that hold a document value (markers) are never left empty.
e. Weather file: USA_NY_Buffalo.Niagara.Intl.AP.725280_TMY3.epw (named on the xlsx sheet "Climate Zone"; the file itself is not in inputs/).
f. Run EnergyPlus 23.1. A pass means no "** Severe **" or "** Fatal **" in eplusout.err and "EnergyPlus Completed Successfully".

3. UNITS AND SCHEDULE RULE
-------------------------
The xlsx sheet "Zone and HVAC System Summary" is in IP units, raw_data is in SI. 1 ft2 = 0.09290304 m2, 1 cfm = 0.000471947 m3/s, 1 W/ft2 = 10.7639104 W/m2, 1 Btu/h-ft2-F = 5.678263 W/m2-K.
Outdoor air per floor area = cfm/ft2 x 0.000471947 / 0.09290304 (m3/s-m2); for example 20 cfm/person x 5.000 people/1000 ft2 = 0.1 cfm/ft2 = 0.000508 m3/s-m2.
Schedules: the xlsx sheet "Schedule Summary" gives 24 hourly values (columns E:AB, 1 am to 12 am) for Mon-Fri, Sat and Sun. In Schedule:Compact they are written as "Until: hh:mm,value" lines; consecutive hours with equal values are joined into one line (the time is the end of the last hour). The day-type lines ("For: ...") and the design-day and holiday lines are not in the xlsx and are kept in the body.

4. WHAT THE DOCUMENTS SUPPLY (the @DOC markers)
---------------------------------------------
271 markers: xlsx sheet "Schedule Summary" (hourly values in Schedule:Compact).
183 markers: xlsx sheet "raw_data" (multiplier, occupancy, lighting and plug densities, infiltration, outdoor air).
0 markers: PDF Tables 24 and 25 (window U-factor and SHGC).
454 markers in total.

Points a new user must know:
- Geometry: the documents give only areas and the window fraction (xlsx raw_data F, J, K; Building Summary R7 = 0.2984). The vertices of all BuildingSurface:Detailed and FenestrationSurface:Detailed objects are not in the documents; they are in the body, relative to the zone origins (the Zone objects give the origins). Appendix_en.md section 1 describes the layout in words and tables.
- Area and PerimeterExposed of Construction:FfactorGroundFloor (and the corresponding Construction:CfactorUndergroundWall values, if the building has them) are computed from the IDF's own polygons (not from the documents) and are plain numbers.
- The F-factor of the slab is the 90.1-2004 uninsulated-slab value; the PDF only says "no insulation required". It is not a marker.
- Window U and SHGC are from the PDF (Tables 24 and 25, Chicago 5A); the xlsx gives only frame-type weights. The wall, roof and floor layers are the prototype's; the documents name the type only.
- Site:Location: the name (city and station) is from the xlsx ("Buffalo", sheet "Climate Zone"); the coordinates and elevation are weather-file header values. The two design days belong to the Buffalo station 725280; for another city they must be regenerated.
- HVAC: the xlsx gives the system type per zone. Sizes, curves, flow rates, set points and node names are the prototype's.

! Vertex formulas of the BuildingSurface:Detailed objects: the coordinates are relative to the origin of the zone of the surface (the Zone objects give the origins), X to the east, Y to the north, Z up. Every coordinate is a length in feet x 0.3048 m/ft x k with k = 0.9999610 (fitted: it makes 4 of the 5 distinct non-zero wall coordinates a whole number of feet within 0.5 mm); coordinates that are not a whole number of feet are written as feet with 4 decimals and marked (position). The NREL PDF has no High-rise Apartment; the exterior walls of the IDF span X = 0.00 to 151.99 ft, Y = 0.00 to 55.50 ft, Z = 0.00 to 100.00 ft (zone origin + coordinate). The prototype generator and the origin offsets are not in the documents.

    SZ DSOA G SW Apartment,  !- Name
    Flow/Area,               !- Outdoor Air Method
    ,                        !- Outdoor Air Flow per Person {m3/s-person}
    @DOC[0.000294105]@;             !- Outdoor Air Flow per Zone Floor Area {m3/s-m2}  Source: xlsx raw_data!P2 Total OSA Ventilation = 0.026 m3/s (= this value x zone floor area, x multiplier for repeated floors); the xlsx lists 0 where the outdoor air is set at the air loop. Derivation of the value: xlsx Zone and HVAC System Summary Q495 (62-1999 cfm/person) = 27.5 cfm/person x 2.105 people per 1000 ft2 (2.00 people in the 949.9 ft2 of the zone) = 0.05789 cfm/ft2 x 0.000471947 m3/s per cfm / 0.09290304 m2 per ft2 = 0.000294105 m3/s-m2; the xlsx does not list the number of people of the outdoor air calculation separately: the people count here is the number that reproduces this result, and it differs from the design occupancy density N495 = 2.632 people per 1000 ft2.

Derivation of the value: xlsx Zone and HVAC System Summary Q496 (62-1999 cfm/person) = 27.5 cfm/person x 2.105 people per 1000 ft2 (2.00 people in the 949.9 ft2 of the zone) = 0.05789 cfm/ft2 x 0.000471947 m3/s per cfm / 0.09290304 m2 per ft2 = 0.000294105 m3/s-m2; the xlsx does not list the number of people of the outdoor air calculation separately: the people count here is the number that reproduces this result, and it differs from the design occupancy density N496 = 2.632 people per 1000 ft2.

Derivation of the value: xlsx Zone and HVAC System Summary Q499 (62-1999 cfm/person) = 27.5 cfm/person x 2.105 people per 1000 ft2 (2.00 people in the 949.9 ft2 of the zone) = 0.05789 cfm/ft2 x 0.000471947 m3/s per cfm / 0.09290304 m2 per ft2 = 0.000294105 m3/s-m2; the xlsx does not list the number of people of the outdoor air calculation separately: the people count here is the number that reproduces this result, and it differs from the design occupancy density N499 = 2.632 people per 1000 ft2.

    Flow/Area,               !- Outdoor Air Method
    ,                        !- Outdoor Air Flow per Person {m3/s-person}
    @DOC[0.000294105]@;             !- Outdoor Air Flow per Zone Floor Area {m3/s-m2}  Source: xlsx raw_data!P7 Total OSA Ventilation = 0.026 m3/s (= this value x zone floor area, x multiplier for repeated floors); the xlsx lists 0 where the outdoor air is set at the air loop. Derivation of the value: xlsx Zone and HVAC System Summary Q500 (62-1999 cfm/person) = 27.5 cfm/person x 2.105 people per 1000 ft2 (2.00 people in the 949.9 ft2 of the zone) = 0.05789 cfm/ft2 x 0.000471947 m3/s per cfm / 0.09290304 m2 per ft2 = 0.000294105 m3/s-m2; the xlsx does not list the number of people of the outdoor air calculation separately: the people count here is the number that reproduces this result, and it differs from the design occupancy density N500 = 2.632 people per 1000 ft2.

Derivation of the value: xlsx Zone and HVAC System Summary Q504 (62-1999 cfm/person) = 27.5 cfm/person x 2.105 people per 1000 ft2 (2.00 people in the 949.9 ft2 of the zone) = 0.05789 cfm/ft2 x 0.000471947 m3/s per cfm / 0.09290304 m2 per ft2 = 0.000294105 m3/s-m2; the xlsx does not list the number of people of the outdoor air calculation separately: the people count here is the number that reproduces this result, and it differs from the design occupancy density N504 = 2.632 people per 1000 ft2.

Derivation of the value: xlsx Zone and HVAC System Summary Q505 (62-1999 cfm/person) = 27.5 cfm/person x 2.105 people per 1000 ft2 (2.00 people in the 949.9 ft2 of the zone) = 0.05789 cfm/ft2 x 0.000471947 m3/s per cfm / 0.09290304 m2 per ft2 = 0.000294105 m3/s-m2; the xlsx does not list the number of people of the outdoor air calculation separately: the people count here is the number that reproduces this result, and it differs from the design occupancy density N505 = 2.632 people per 1000 ft2.

    ,                        !- Outdoor Air Flow per Person {m3/s-person}
    @DOC[0.000294105]@;             !- Outdoor Air Flow per Zone Floor Area {m3/s-m2}  Source: xlsx raw_data!P13 Total OSA Ventilation = 0.2076 m3/s (= this value x zone floor area, x multiplier for repeated floors); the xlsx lists 0 where the outdoor air is set at the air loop. Derivation of the value: xlsx Zone and HVAC System Summary Q506 (62-1999 cfm/person) = 27.5 cfm/person x 2.105 people per 1000 ft2 (2.00 people in the 949.9 ft2 of the zone) = 0.05789 cfm/ft2 x 0.000471947 m3/s per cfm / 0.09290304 m2 per ft2 = 0.000294105 m3/s-m2; the xlsx does not list the number of people of the outdoor air calculation separately: the people count here is the number that reproduces this result, and it differs from the design occupancy density N506 = 2.632 people per 1000 ft2.

Derivation of the value: xlsx Zone and HVAC System Summary Q507 (62-1999 cfm/person) = 27.5 cfm/person x 2.105 people per 1000 ft2 (2.00 people in the 949.9 ft2 of the zone) = 0.05789 cfm/ft2 x 0.000471947 m3/s per cfm / 0.09290304 m2 per ft2 = 0.000294105 m3/s-m2; the xlsx does not list the number of people of the outdoor air calculation separately: the people count here is the number that reproduces this result, and it differs from the design occupancy density N507 = 2.632 people per 1000 ft2.

Derivation of the value: xlsx Zone and HVAC System Summary Q509 (62-1999 cfm/person) = 27.5 cfm/person x 2.105 people per 1000 ft2 (2.00 people in the 949.9 ft2 of the zone) = 0.05789 cfm/ft2 x 0.000471947 m3/s per cfm / 0.09290304 m2 per ft2 = 0.000294105 m3/s-m2; the xlsx does not list the number of people of the outdoor air calculation separately: the people count here is the number that reproduces this result, and it differs from the design occupancy density N509 = 2.632 people per 1000 ft2.

Derivation of the value: xlsx Zone and HVAC System Summary Q510 (62-1999 cfm/person) = 27.5 cfm/person x 2.105 people per 1000 ft2 (2.00 people in the 949.9 ft2 of the zone) = 0.05789 cfm/ft2 x 0.000471947 m3/s per cfm / 0.09290304 m2 per ft2 = 0.000294105 m3/s-m2; the xlsx does not list the number of people of the outdoor air calculation separately: the people count here is the number that reproduces this result, and it differs from the design occupancy density N510 = 2.632 people per 1000 ft2.

    ,                        !- Outdoor Air Flow per Person {m3/s-person}
    @DOC[0.000294105]@;             !- Outdoor Air Flow per Zone Floor Area {m3/s-m2}  Source: xlsx raw_data!P18 Total OSA Ventilation = 0.2076 m3/s (= this value x zone floor area, x multiplier for repeated floors); the xlsx lists 0 where the outdoor air is set at the air loop. Derivation of the value: xlsx Zone and HVAC System Summary Q511 (62-1999 cfm/person) = 27.5 cfm/person x 2.105 people per 1000 ft2 (2.00 people in the 949.9 ft2 of the zone) = 0.05789 cfm/ft2 x 0.000471947 m3/s per cfm / 0.09290304 m2 per ft2 = 0.000294105 m3/s-m2; the xlsx does not list the number of people of the outdoor air calculation separately: the people count here is the number that reproduces this result, and it differs from the design occupancy density N511 = 2.632 people per 1000 ft2.

Derivation of the value: xlsx Zone and HVAC System Summary Q513 (62-1999 cfm/person) = 27.5 cfm/person x 2.105 people per 1000 ft2 (2.00 people in the 949.9 ft2 of the zone) = 0.05789 cfm/ft2 x 0.000471947 m3/s per cfm / 0.09290304 m2 per ft2 = 0.000294105 m3/s-m2; the xlsx does not list the number of people of the outdoor air calculation separately: the people count here is the number that reproduces this result, and it differs from the design occupancy density N513 = 2.632 people per 1000 ft2.

Derivation of the value: xlsx Zone and HVAC System Summary Q515 (62-1999 cfm/person) = 27.5 cfm/person x 2.105 people per 1000 ft2 (2.00 people in the 949.9 ft2 of the zone) = 0.05789 cfm/ft2 x 0.000471947 m3/s per cfm / 0.09290304 m2 per ft2 = 0.000294105 m3/s-m2; the xlsx does not list the number of people of the outdoor air calculation separately: the people count here is the number that reproduces this result, and it differs from the design occupancy density N515 = 2.632 people per 1000 ft2.

Derivation of the value: xlsx Zone and HVAC System Summary Q516 (62-1999 cfm/person) = 27.5 cfm/person x 2.105 people per 1000 ft2 (2.00 people in the 949.9 ft2 of the zone) = 0.05789 cfm/ft2 x 0.000471947 m3/s per cfm / 0.09290304 m2 per ft2 = 0.000294105 m3/s-m2; the xlsx does not list the number of people of the outdoor air calculation separately: the people count here is the number that reproduces this result, and it differs from the design occupancy density N516 = 2.632 people per 1000 ft2.

    @DOC[0.000294105]@;             !- Outdoor Air Flow per Zone Floor Area {m3/s-m2}  Source: xlsx raw_data!P26 Total OSA Ventilation = 0.026 m3/s (= this value x zone floor area, x multiplier for repeated floors); the xlsx lists 0 where the outdoor air is set at the air loop. Derivation of the value: xlsx Zone and HVAC System Summary Q519 (62-1999 cfm/person) = 27.5 cfm/person x 2.105 people per 1000 ft2 (2.00 people in the 949.9 ft2 of the zone) = 0.05789 cfm/ft2 x 0.000471947 m3/s per cfm / 0.09290304 m2 per ft2 = 0.000294105 m3/s-m2; the xlsx does not list the number of people of the outdoor air calculation separately: the people count here is the number that reproduces this result, and it differs from the design occupancy density N519 = 2.632 people per 1000 ft2.

Derivation of the value: xlsx Zone and HVAC System Summary Q520 (62-1999 cfm/person) = 27.5 cfm/person x 2.105 people per 1000 ft2 (2.00 people in the 949.9 ft2 of the zone) = 0.05789 cfm/ft2 x 0.000471947 m3/s per cfm / 0.09290304 m2 per ft2 = 0.000294105 m3/s-m2; the xlsx does not list the number of people of the outdoor air calculation separately: the people count here is the number that reproduces this result, and it differs from the design occupancy density N520 = 2.632 people per 1000 ft2.

! [moved to Appendix_en.md section 5.6: Schedule:Compact]
  EnergyManagementSystem:GlobalVariable,
    BoilerType,              !- Erl Variable 1 Name
    CapRated,                !- Erl Variable 2 Name
    EtRated,                 !- Erl Variable 3 Name
    PLRx,                    !- <none>
    Ecx,                     !- <none>
    SkinLossRate;            !- <none>

  EnergyManagementSystem:Sensor,
    BoilerPLR,               !- Name
    Central Boiler,          !- Output:Variable or Output:Meter Index Key Name
    Boiler Part Load Ratio;  !- Output:Variable or Output:Meter Name

  EnergyManagementSystem:Actuator,
    BoilerCurve,             !- Name
    Central BOILER EFFICIENCY CURVE,  !- Actuated Component Unique Name
    Curve,                   !- Actuated Component Type
    Curve Result;            !- Actuated Component Control Type

  EnergyManagementSystem:Program,
    SetBoilerPar,            !- Name
    SET CapRated =  622288.49,  !- Program Line 1
    SET EtRated =  0.75,     !- Program Line 2
    SET CapRated = (CapRated *3.412)/1000,  !- <none>
    SET SkinLossRate = 0.02, !- <none>
    SET BoilerType = 2,      !- <none>
    IF BoilerType  == 1,     !- <none>
    IF CapRated >10000,      !- <none>
    SET PLRx = 0.2,          !- <none>
    ELSEIF CapRated >5000,   !- <none>
    SET PLRx = 0.25,         !- <none>
    ELSEIF CapRated >1000,   !- <none>
    SET PLRx = 0.33,         !- <none>
    ELSE,                    !- <none>
    SET BoilerType = 2,      !- <none>
    SET PLRx = 0.2,          !- <none>
    ENDIF,                   !- <none>
    ELSE,                    !- <none>
    SET PLRx = 0.2,          !- <none>
    ENDIF,                   !- <none>
    IF BoilerType == 1,      !- <none>
    SET CurveX = 0.975 + 0.305 * PLRx - 0.527 * (PLRx^2) + 0.249 * (PLRx^3),  !- <none>
    ELSEIF BoilerType == 2,  !- <none>
    SET CurveX = 0.907 + 0.320 * PLRx - 0.420 * (PLRx^2) + 0.193 * (PLRx^3),  !- <none>
    ELSE,                    !- <none>
    SET CurveX = 0.492 + 1.41 * PLRx - 1.529 * (PLRx^2) + 0.628 * (PLRx^3),  !- <none>
    ENDIF,                   !- <none>
    SET LoadX = CapRated * PLRx,  !- <none>
    SET Etx = CurveX * EtRated,  !- <none>
    SET InputEx = LoadX/Etx, !- <none>
    SET SkinEx = CapRated * SkinLossRate,  !- <none>
    SET Ecx = (LoadX + SkinEx)/InputEx;  !- <none>

  EnergyManagementSystem:Program,
    SetBoilerCurve,          !- Name
    IF BoilerPLR >= PLRx,    !- Program Line 1
    IF BoilerType == 1,      !- Program Line 2
    SET BoilerCurve = 0.975 + 0.305*BoilerPLR - 0.527*(BoilerPLR^2) + 0.249*(BoilerPLR^3),  !- <none>
    ELSEIF BoilerType == 2,  !- <none>
    SET BoilerCurve = 0.907 + 0.320*BoilerPLR - 0.420*(BoilerPLR^2) + 0.193*(BoilerPLR^3),  !- <none>
    ELSE,                    !- <none>
    SET BoilerCurve = 0.492 + 1.41*BoilerPLR - 1.529*(BoilerPLR^2) + 0.628*(BoilerPLR^3),  !- <none>
    ENDIF,                   !- <none>
    ELSE,                    !- <none>
    IF BoilerPLR == 0,       !- <none>
    SET BoilerCurve = NULL,  !- <none>
    ELSE,                    !- <none>
    IF BoilerPLR <0.05,      !- <none>
    SET BoilerCurve = (0.05/(0.05 + SkinLossRate)) * (Ecx/EtRated),  !- <none>
    ELSE,                    !- <none>
    SET BoilerCurve = (BoilerPLR/(BoilerPLR + SkinLossRate)) * (Ecx/EtRated),  !- <none>
    ENDIF,                   !- <none>
    ENDIF,                   !- <none>
    ENDIF;                   !- <none>

  EnergyManagementSystem:ProgramCallingManager,
    BoilerParManager,        !- Name
    BeginNewEnvironment,     !- EnergyPlus Model Calling Point
    SetBoilerPar;            !- Program Name 1

  EnergyManagementSystem:ProgramCallingManager,
    BoilerCurveManager,      !- Name
    InsideHVACSystemIterationLoop,  !- EnergyPlus Model Calling Point
    SetBoilerCurve;          !- Program Name 1

  Output:Meter:MeterFileOnly,Electricity:Facility,Hourly;

  Output:Meter:MeterFileOnly,ElectricityNet:Facility,Hourly;

  Output:Meter:MeterFileOnly,Gas:Facility,Hourly;
